The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 2 is 0.33. The other faces contribute 0.50 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.33+0.50)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.14</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.50&minus;0.33 =</span> <strong>0.17</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.50+0 =</span> <strong>0.5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.33+0.50 =</span> <strong>0.83</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 1 is 0.08. The other faces contribute 0.75 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.08+0.75)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.14</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.75&minus;0.08 =</span> <strong>0.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.75+0 =</span> <strong>0.75</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.08+0.75 =</span> <strong>0.83</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 1?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;5)/5 =</span> <strong>-1</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;5)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>4.17</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;5)<sup>2</sup>/5 =</span> <strong>5</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;5)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>25</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 1 is 0.20. The other faces contribute 2.70 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.20+2.70)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.48</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>2.70&minus;0.20 =</span> <strong>2.5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>2.70+0 =</span> <strong>2.7</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.20+2.70 =</span> <strong>2.9</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 4?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(9&minus;8)<sup>2</sup>/9 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.11</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(9&minus;8)<sup>2</sup>/8 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.12</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(9&minus;8)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.17</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(9&minus;8)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>1</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 1 is 0.80. The other faces contribute 10.80 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.80+10.80)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>1.93</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>10.80&minus;0.80 =</span> <strong>10</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>10.80+0 =</span> <strong>10.8</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.80+10.80 =</span> <strong>11.6</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 1?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12&minus;10)/10 =</span> <strong>0.2</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/12 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.33</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/10 =</span> <strong>0.4</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12&minus;10)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>4</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 3?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;10)/10 =</span> <strong>-1</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/10 =</span> <strong>10</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>16.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;10)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>100</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 3 is 15.00. The other faces contribute 15.00 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>15.00&minus;15.00 =</span> <strong>0</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(15.00+15.00)/6 =</span> <strong>5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>15.00+0 =</span> <strong>15</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>15.00+15.00 =</span> <strong>30</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 3 is 5.00. The other faces contribute 5.00 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>5.00&minus;5.00 =</span> <strong>0</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(5.00+5.00)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>1.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>5.00+0 =</span> <strong>5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>5.00+5.00 =</span> <strong>10</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 1 is 3.20. The other faces contribute 8.40 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(3.20+8.40)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>1.93</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>8.40&minus;3.20 =</span> <strong>5.2</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>8.40+0 =</span> <strong>8.4</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>3.20+8.40 =</span> <strong>11.6</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 4 is 0.50. The other faces contribute 6.75 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.50+6.75)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>1.21</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>6.75&minus;0.50 =</span> <strong>6.25</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>6.75+0 =</span> <strong>6.75</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.50+6.75 =</span> <strong>7.25</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 5 is 0.17. The other faces contribute 1.50 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.17+1.50)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.28</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>1.50&minus;0.17 =</span> <strong>1.33</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>1.50+0 =</span> <strong>1.5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.17+1.50 =</span> <strong>1.67</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 6 is 0.80. The other faces contribute 10.80 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.80+10.80)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>1.93</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>10.80&minus;0.80 =</span> <strong>10</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>10.80+0 =</span> <strong>10.8</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.80+10.80 =</span> <strong>11.6</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 1?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;10)/10 =</span> <strong>-1</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/10 =</span> <strong>10</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>16.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;10)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>100</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 2 is 0.33. The other faces contribute 0.50 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.33+0.50)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.14</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.50&minus;0.33 =</span> <strong>0.17</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.50+0 =</span> <strong>0.5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.33+0.50 =</span> <strong>0.83</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 2?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12&minus;6)/6 =</span> <strong>1</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12&minus;6)<sup>2</sup>/12 =</span> <strong>3</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12&minus;6)<sup>2</sup>/6 =</span> <strong>6</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12&minus;6)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>36</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 5 is 0.40. The other faces contribute 5.40 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.40+5.40)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.97</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>5.40&minus;0.40 =</span> <strong>5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>5.40+0 =</span> <strong>5.4</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.40+5.40 =</span> <strong>5.8</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 1 is 12.00. The other faces contribute 12.00 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>12.00&minus;12.00 =</span> <strong>0</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12.00+12.00)/6 =</span> <strong>4</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>12.00+0 =</span> <strong>12</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>12.00+12.00 =</span> <strong>24</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 5?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(2&minus;5)/5 =</span> <strong>-0.6</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(2&minus;5)<sup>2</sup>/6 =</span> <strong>1.5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(2&minus;5)<sup>2</sup>/5 =</span> <strong>1.8</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(2&minus;5)<sup>2</sup>/2 =</span> <strong>4.5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(2&minus;5)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>9</strong>	Incorrect
